<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We determine the limiting free energy of the Ising perceptron with independent Gaussian patterns for every bounded Borel log-potential, at every fixed positive temperature and pattern density. We give an explicit variational formula for the limit and prove convergence in expectation and probability.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): For every $\alpha>0$ and every bounded Borel $f:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R$, $$\label{thm:formula} \lim_{N\to\infty}\mathbb E p_N(f) =\mathcal P_f(\alpha) :=\inf_{q\in\mathcal Q}\{\alpha V_f(q)+S_{\mathrm I}(q)\}.$$ Moreover, $p_N(f)$ converges to $\mathcal P_f(\alpha)$ in probability. The variational value is finite. In particular, the conclusion holds for $f=\beta\phi$ at every $\beta>0$ and every bounded continuous activation $\phi$, without symmetry, concavity, or a smallness restriction.
  - Explanation: The limiting Gaussian pressure is a finite variational infimum over overlap paths, with convergence in expectation and probability.
- **C2** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-var-variation)): On a fixed partition with $w_i=\zeta_{i+1}-\zeta_i$, the first variations along every feasible line segment of step paths are $$\begin{aligned} \label{var:pattern-variation} \frac{d}{da}V_f(q+a\dot q) &=-\frac12\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}w_i\dot q_i\, \mathbb E[U_i'(Z_i)^2],\\ \label{var:field-variation} \frac{d}{da}\ell(h+a\dot h) &=-\frac12\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}w_i\dot h_i\, \mathbb E[U_i'(Z_i)^2]. \end{aligned}$$ The functions and chains on the right are computed at the current path. Endpoint derivatives are understood from within the segment. The squared-derivative expectations on the right are continuous on the closed finite-dimensional parameter cones. In both recursions these expectations are nonnegative and nondecreasing in $i$. In the field recursion, define $$\label{var:rho} \rho_h(u)=\rho_i:=\mathbb E[U_i'(Z_i)^2], \qquad \zeta_i<u<\zeta_{i+1}.$$ Then $0\le\rho_h\le1$, and subdivision does not change this path.
  - Explanation: Feasible path variations are weighted squared derivatives along the tilted chain; the field derivative defines an ordered bounded overlap path.
- **C3** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-var-continuity-order)): For step paths on arbitrary partitions, $$\begin{aligned} \label{var:lipschitz} \|V_f(p)-V_f(q)\|&\le\tfrac12\\|f'\\|_\infty^2\\|p-q\\|_{L^1},\\ \|\ell(h')-\ell(h)\|&\le\tfrac12\\|h'-h\\|_{L^1}. \end{aligned}$$ Thus $V_f$ extends uniquely and continuously to all nondecreasing $[0,1]$-valued paths, and $\ell$ extends to all bounded nonnegative nondecreasing paths. Moreover, $$\label{var:tail-order} \int_s^1p(u)\,du\ge\int_s^1q(u)\,du \quad\hbox{for every }s\in[0,1] \quad\Longrightarrow\quad V_f(p)\le V_f(q).$$
  - Explanation: The recursion values extend continuously in path distance, and ordered tail integrals reverse the order of the pattern functional.
- **C4** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-var-step-reduction)): The entropy in Definition <a href="#def:entropy" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:entropy">2</a> is nonnegative, nondecreasing in pointwise path order, and satisfies $S_{\mathrm I}(0)=0$. The variational infimum is finite, and $$\label{var:step-infimum} \inf_q\{\alpha V_f(q)+S_{\mathrm I}(q)\} =\inf_{\substack{q\text{ step}\\q_{k-1}<1}} \{\alpha V_f(q)+S_{\mathrm I}(q)\}.$$
  - Explanation: The entropy is nonnegative and ordered, and the variational infimum can be restricted to finite step trials with top value below one.
- **C5** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-var-folded-kernel)): Suppose $U:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R$ is even and convex. Let $K$ be its tilted Gaussian kernel with variance $s\ge0$ and coefficient $d\ge0$, and assume its normalizer $\mathbb E e^{dU(x+\sqrt sG)}$ is finite for every $x\in\mathbb R$. If $A$ is bounded, even, and nondecreasing in $\|x\|$, then $KA$ has these properties as well. The same statement with “nonincreasing” holds with both monotonicities reversed.
  - Explanation: A normalized folded Gaussian transition preserves evenness and monotonicity in absolute value under the stated integrability condition.
- **C6** (theorem, [source](#stmt-var-concavity)): The functional $\ell$ is concave on nonnegative nondecreasing step paths. For any two such paths, represented on a common partition, $$\label{var:support} \ell(h')\le\ell(h) -\frac12\int_0^1(h'(u)-h(u))\rho_h(u)\,du.$$ Concavity also holds for its continuous extension to bounded nonnegative nondecreasing paths.
  - Explanation: Concavity of the field recursion gives a global supporting inequality and extends to bounded ordered fields.
- **C7** (theorem, [source](#stmt-arr-ultrametric)): Suppose $B$ is a bounded, symmetric, weakly exchangeable Gram array with constant diagonal $D$ and the Ghirlanda–Guerra property. Then, almost surely, for distinct indices, $$B_{\ell m}\geq0, \qquad B_{12}\geq\min\{B_{13},B_{23}\}. \label{arr:ultrametric-inequality}$$
  - Explanation: The array hypotheses force nonnegative off-diagonal overlaps and ultrametricity almost surely.
- **C8** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-arr-cascade)): The total $T$ in <a href="#arr:cascade-weights" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="arr:cascade-weights">[arr:cascade-weights]</a> is almost surely finite and positive, with $\mathbb E[(\log T)^2]<\infty$. For replicas sampled from $(v_\gamma)$, the common-level array satisfies the Ghirlanda–Guerra property and $$\mathbb P(\gamma^1\wedge\gamma^2=i)=w_i, \qquad 0\leq i\leq k-1. \label{arr:level-marginal}$$ Conversely, a weakly exchangeable ultrametric scalar array with the Ghirlanda–Guerra property and finitely many off-diagonal values has the same off-diagonal law as this cascade, with the levels replaced by its ordered values and with $w_i$ their marginal probabilities. Values of zero probability are omitted.
  - Explanation: The Poisson cascade is well defined and its sampled ancestral levels realize the prescribed marginal and overlap identities; the finite-level law is unique.
- **C9** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-arr-quantization)): Let $B$ be nonnegative and ultrametric off the diagonal, with constant diagonal $D$ and entries at most $D$. Applying any nonnegative nondecreasing finite-valued map to its off-diagonal entries, with image contained in $[0,D]$, and retaining diagonal $D$, produces a Gram array. If $B$ has the Ghirlanda–Guerra property, its quantized off-diagonal law is the finite cascade law in Lemma <a href="#arr:cascade" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="arr:cascade">11</a>.* *More generally, suppose finitely many desired off-diagonal covariance entries are bounded nonnegative nondecreasing functions $a_j(r)$ of the scalar entries $r=B_{\ell m}$, $\ell\ne m$, with $a_j(r)\leq D_j$. One can choose finite ordered bins of $r$ that approximate $r$ and every $a_j(r)$ uniformly on their domain, with errors tending to zero, while keeping each covariance diagonal equal to $D_j$. All the resulting covariance arrays are Gram. The binning can be chosen as a function of $r$ alone, so it never splits an atom of the scalar overlap law.
  - Explanation: Ordered finite quantization preserves the Gram property while retaining the original diagonal, including joint ordered covariance coordinates.
- **C10** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-arr-law-continuity)): For fixed diagonal $D$, the law of a nonnegative ultrametric Ghirlanda–Guerra array with values in $[0,D]$ is determined by its two-replica marginal. Such a law exists for every probability marginal on $[0,D]$. If the marginal quantiles $q_m,q$ satisfy $\\|q_m-q\\|_{L^1(0,1)}\to0$, then the laws of every fixed finite replica block converge in bounded-Lipschitz distance.
  - Explanation: The overlap marginal determines the array law, and convergence of quantiles implies convergence of fixed finite replica restrictions.
- **C11** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-arr-tilt)): Let $X_{k-1}$ be a terminal log factor depending on the marked path. For $i\ge1$, let $\mathbb E_i$ integrate the original level-$i$ child-mark kernel, conditional on the marked ancestor path through level $i-1$, without averaging the Poisson weights or root data. Define $$X_{i-1}=\frac1{\zeta_i}\log\mathbb E_i e^{\zeta_i X_i}, \qquad i=k-1,\ldots,1. \label{arr:mark-recursion}$$ Assume these transforms are finite; the bounded log factors and the Gaussian log factors with at most linear growth used below satisfy this condition. For $Z=\sum_\gamma v_\gamma e^{X_{k-1}(\gamma)}$, $$\mathbb E[\log Z\mid\text{root data}]=X_0, \qquad \mathbb E[(\log Z-X_0)^2\mid\text{root data}] \leq4\mathbb E[(\log T)^2]. \label{arr:log-cascade}$$ Here $T$ is the unmarked total in <a href="#arr:cascade-weights" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="arr:cascade-weights">[arr:cascade-weights]</a>.* *After reweighting by $e^{X_{k-1}}$ and relabeling children, the joint law of normalized weights and marks can be generated by first drawing an ordinary unmarked cascade, independently of the root data, and then drawing marks on its vertices with kernels of densities $$e^{\zeta_i(X_i-X_{i-1})},\qquad 1\leq i\leq k-1, \label{arr:tilted-kernel}$$ relative to the original kernels, with independent draws on distinct children conditional on ancestor marks. Root data retain their original law. In particular, sampling reweighted leaves preserves their shape law; conditioning on that shape leaves exactly these mark kernels along the sampled branches. Independent mark components remain independent under these kernels when their original kernels factor and the terminal log factor is a sum of the separate component log factors.
  - Explanation: Cascade reweighting changes the conditional mark kernels while preserving the unmarked shape law and the original root law.
- **C12** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-arr-limit)): Let a sequence of random base probabilities have jointly convergent arrays of bounded observables and of the conditional covariances of finitely many fresh centered Gaussian fields. The fields are sampled independently of the base probability conditional on its data, with any specified independence between field components. Then the following averages converge, as determined by those limiting arrays:* 1. *bounded continuous functions of the listed observable and field evaluations on finitely many base replicas;* 2. *the same tests after reweighting by a fixed continuous function of the listed evaluations at one replica, bounded between two fixed positive constants;* 3. *the expected logarithm of the normalizing integral of that factor.* *Independent finite-spin averages may be included. The assertion remains valid at singular limiting covariance matrices. If the limiting off-diagonal covariance entries are ordered functions of a scalar ultrametric Ghirlanda–Guerra array, its evaluations can be obtained by the quantized cascades of Lemma <a href="#arr:quantization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="arr:quantization">12</a>, retaining the true diagonal variances and using <a href="#arr:residual-average" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="arr:residual-average">[arr:residual-average]</a>, provided the base observables and tests used in the evaluation are also approximated by these bins in probability on every finite replica restriction. This condition includes constants and continuous tests of the scalar overlap and the quantized covariance coordinates.
  - Explanation: Joint array convergence controls fresh Gaussian tests and bounded positive reweightings, including normalized expectations and logarithms.
- **C13** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-arr-fresh-pattern)): Suppose that the limiting spin-overlap array of a sequence of base Gibbs probabilities has diagonal one, is Gram, and satisfies the Ghirlanda–Guerra property. Let $p$ be the quantile of its off-diagonal two-replica law. For a fresh independent Gaussian pattern field $g(x)$ with conditional covariance $R(x,x')$, and bounded continuous $f$, $$\lim\mathbb E\log\langle e^{f(g(x))}\rangle=V_f(p), \label{arr:fresh-pattern-value}$$ whenever $V_f$ is understood through its continuous quantile extension. In particular this applies to the smooth potentials used in the proof before the final approximation step.
  - Explanation: A fresh independent Gaussian pattern contributes the pattern recursion evaluated at the limiting overlap quantile.
- **C14** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-arr-calculus)): Let $P$ be a probability reference measure, possibly random but independent of the Gaussian fields under consideration. For independent centered Gaussian energies with covariances $C_0,C_1$, interpolate using $H_t=\sqrt{1-t}\,H_0+\sqrt t\,H_1$, and put $\Delta=C_1-C_0$. Then $$\frac{d}{dt}\mathbb E\log P(e^{H_t}) =\frac12\mathbb E\langle\Delta_{11}-\Delta_{12}\rangle_t. \label{arr:gaussian-log-derivative}$$ For a bounded $r$-replica test $F$ independent of these fields, $$\begin{aligned} \frac{d}{dt}\mathbb E\langle F\rangle_t =\frac12\mathbb E\Bigg\langle F\Bigg( &\sum_{a,b=1}^r\Delta_{ab} -2r\sum_{a=1}^r\Delta_{a,r+1}\notag\\ &+r(r+1)\Delta_{r+1,r+2}-r\Delta_{r+1,r+1} \Bigg)\Bigg\rangle_t, \label{arr:replica-derivative} \end{aligned}$$ so its magnitude is at most $(2r^2+r)\\|F\\|_\infty\\|\Delta\\|_\infty$. If an independent centered Gaussian field $Y$ of covariance $C$ enters the energy with coefficient $s$, Gaussian integration by parts also gives $$\mathbb E\langle Y(1)F\rangle =s\,\mathbb E\left\langle F\left(\sum_{a=1}^r C_{1a}-rC_{1,r+1}\right) \right\rangle. \label{arr:gaussian-insertion}$$ These formulas hold for finite spin spaces with countably many cascade labels whenever the covariances are bounded at the fixed system size. They can be integrated up to the endpoints of covariance segments.
  - Explanation: Gaussian differentiation expresses changes of log normalizers and replica expectations through covariance differences.
- **C15** (bound, [source](#stmt-arr-variance)): A function of finite standard Gaussian data with Euclidean Lipschitz constant $A$ has variance at most $A^2$. A function of independent inputs whose value changes by at most $c_i$ when input $i$ is replaced has variance at most $\sum_i c_i^2$. If a function $a$ on the integers has Lipschitz constant $c$ and $K$ is Poisson with mean $\lambda$, then $\operatorname{Var}(a(K))\leq c^2\lambda$.
  - Explanation: Variance is controlled by Gaussian Lipschitz constants, bounded independent-input changes, or Poisson Lipschitz increments.
- **C16** (bound, [source](#stmt-upper-bound)): For every nondecreasing $[0,1]$-valued path $q$, $$\label{upper:conclusion} \limsup_{N\to\infty}\mathbb E p_N \le \alpha V_f(q)+S_{\mathrm I}(q).$$ Consequently the limit superior is bounded above by the variational infimum in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">3</a>.
  - Explanation: Every admissible trial overlap path bounds the limiting expected pressure from above.
- **C17** (bound, [source](#stmt-upper-concentration)): For each fixed $H<\infty$ there is a constant $C_H$, also depending on the fixed partition, $\alpha$, and $f$, such that $$\label{upper:variance} \operatorname{Var}(P_N(t,h,u))\le\frac{C_H}{N}$$ whenever $0\le t\le\alpha$, $0\le h_0\le\cdots\le h_{k-1}\le H$, and $u\in[1,2]^N$.
  - Explanation: The enriched pressure concentrates uniformly when the field path is capped and the other parameters remain in their stated ranges.
- **C18** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-upper-derivatives)): The function $F_N$ is continuous on the parameter region above. For every nonnegative nondecreasing direction $\lambda$ on the fixed partition, its right directional derivative in $h$ is $$\label{upper:field-derivative} \partial_{h,\lambda}F_N =\frac12\mathbb E\langle R_{12}\lambda_{\gamma^1\wedge\gamma^2}\rangle.$$ For $t>0$, its time derivative is $$\label{upper:time-derivative} \partial_tF_N =-\mathbb E\log\left\langle \exp f(\widehat g\cdot x/\sqrt N)\right\rangle,$$ where $\widehat g$ is an independent standard Gaussian pattern.
  - Explanation: Field derivatives probe spin overlaps, while the density derivative adds a fresh nonlinear pattern.
- **C19** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-upper-coercive)): For the fixed partition, $$\label{upper:field-growth} \ell(h)\le (\mathbb E\|G\|)\sqrt{h_0}+C_{\zeta} -\frac12\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}w_ih_i.$$ For sufficiently large fixed $H$, every sufficiently large dimension in the violating sequence has a minimizer of <a href="#upper:objective" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="upper:objective">[upper:objective]</a> satisfying $t>0$ and $h_{k-1}<H$.
  - Explanation: The field growth estimate excludes the field-cap face and the initial-time face at a violating comparison minimum.
- **C20** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-upper-gg)): Every subsequential limit of the sampled joint arrays $(R,T)$ at these deterministic minimizing triples satisfies the joint Ghirlanda–Guerra identities of Definition <a href="#arr:gg" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="arr:gg">9</a>.
  - Explanation: Deterministic minimizing perturbations enforce joint spin and cascade overlap identities in every subsequential array limit.
- **C21** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-upper-synchronization)): In any joint array limit from Lemma <a href="#upper:gg" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="upper:gg">23</a>, the two-replica law of $(R_{12},T_{12})$ is supported on a set ordered in both coordinates. If $p$ is the quantile of $R_{12}$, the conditional law of $R_{12}$ given $T_{12}=i/k$ is the law of $p(U)$ for $U$ uniform on $(\zeta_i,\zeta_{i+1})$.
  - Explanation: The limiting spin and tree overlap support is jointly ordered, identifying conditional overlap laws with quantile blocks.
- **C22** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-bulk-good-parameters)): There are measurable sets $\mathcal G_N\subset[1,2]^{\mathbb N}$ with $\pi(\mathcal G_N)\to1$ such that the following holds. For every deterministic sequence $v(N)\in\mathcal G_N$, every subsequential limit of the omitted system’s sampled overlap array satisfies the Ghirlanda–Guerra identities of Definition <a href="#arr:gg" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="arr:gg">9</a>.
  - Explanation: Uniform good parameter sets ensure overlap identities for arbitrary deterministic choices of parameters from those sets.
- **C23** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-bulk-restoration)): Start with any deterministic good parameter sequence, on any subsequence of dimensions. Pass to a further subsequence on which the omitted overlap array converges and the full system’s joint sampled arrays $(R_{lm},B_N(x^l,x^m),C_N(x^l))$ converge. Let $\mu$ be the omitted limit’s two-replica overlap law. The full limiting overlap array has the same law as the omitted one. There are deterministic constants $a_{\mathrm d},c$ and a deterministic nondecreasing function $a$, defined on a $\mu$-full ordered subset of $[0,1]$, such that almost surely $$\label{bulk:deterministic-covariances} B_{lm}=a(R_{lm})\quad(l\ne m),\qquad B_{ll}=a_{\mathrm d},\qquad C_l=c, \qquad 0\le a\le a_{\mathrm d}\le\alpha\\|f'\\|_\infty^2.$$ Moreover, $$\label{bulk:ibp-balance} c+a_{\mathrm d}=\int r\,a(r)\,\mu(dr).$$
  - Explanation: Restoring omitted patterns preserves the limiting overlap law and identifies deterministic ordered gradient covariances and a cavity correction balance.
- **C24** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cav-finite-comparison)): Let $$\label{cav:untilted-base} G_0=G_N^v\otimes\nu_L$$ be the size-$N$ bulk Gibbs probability times the independent cavity-spin prior, *before* multiplication by $W$. There is a deterministic $\kappa_L>0$ such that $G_0(W)\ge\kappa_L$ for every realization and all sufficiently large $N$. Uniformly over all allowed $v$, $$\label{cav:finite-increment} \Delta_{N,L}(v)=\mathbb E\log G_0(W)+o(1).$$ The size-$(N+L)$ Gibbs expectations of any fixed bounded finite-replica spin test are likewise approximated, after the above coupling, by the measure $W\,dG_0/G_0(W)$, with disorder-averaged error $o(1)$ times the test’s bound. In particular this applies to bounded continuous functions of $R^+_{12}$ and their products with $z_1^1z_1^2$; inside such continuous tests one may also replace $R^+_{12}$ by $R_{12}$. Finally, $\Delta_{N,L}(v)\ge-C_L$ uniformly for large $N$.
  - Explanation: The untilted cavity factor has a deterministic positive lower bound; the normalized cavity approximation controls increments and replica tests.
- **C25** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-cav-dual-attainment)): For every subsequential pair $(p,h)$ just constructed, $$\label{cav:entropy-identity} S_{\mathrm I}(p)=\ell(h)+\frac12\int_0^1p(u)h(u)\,du.$$ The right side uses the continuous extension of $\ell$ to bounded nondecreasing fields.
  - Explanation: The bounded field produced by the cavity limit attains the dual entropy supremum at the same overlap quantile.
- **C26** (bound, [source](#stmt-cav-lower-bound)): For $f\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R)$, $$\liminf_{N\to\infty}\mathbb E p_N \ge\inf_q\{S_{\mathrm I}(q)+\alpha V_f(q)\}.$$
  - Explanation: Cavity increments provide the matching variational lower bound for smooth compactly supported potentials.
- **C27** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-ext-path-approx)): If $f,g$ are bounded Borel functions with $\|f\|,\|g\|\le K$, then $$\begin{aligned} \big\|\mathbb E p_N(f)-\mathbb E p_N(g)\big\| &\le\alpha e^{2K}\\|f-g\\|_{L^1(\gamma)},\label{ext:pressure-distance}\\ \sup_{q\text{ step}}\|V_f(q)-V_g(q)\| &\le e^{2K}\\|f-g\\|_{L^1(\gamma)}.\label{ext:path-distance} \end{aligned}$$ Consequently, for each bounded Borel $f$, the pattern functional $V_f$ extends uniquely and continuously in $L^1$ from step paths to $\mathcal Q$, and the bound in <a href="#ext:path-distance" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:path-distance">[ext:path-distance]</a> holds on all of $\mathcal Q$.
  - Explanation: Gaussian integrable approximation of bounded potentials controls the pressure and pattern functional uniformly and defines the continuous path extension.
- **C28** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-ext-bounded)): Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">3</a> holds for every bounded Borel activation.
  - Explanation: The smooth formula extends to bounded Borel activations.
- **C29** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ext-integrable)): Suppose $f:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R$ is Borel and $$\label{ext:moments} \mathbb E\|f(G)\|+\mathbb E e^{f(G)}<\infty.$$ For $J,K>0$, let $f_{J,K}=\min\{K,\max\{-J,f\}\}$. The uniform limit $$\label{ext:unbounded-V} V_f=\lim_{J\to\infty}\lim_{K\to\infty}V_{f_{J,K}} \quad\text{on }\mathcal Q$$ exists and is continuous in $L^1$. With this definition, the variational formula <a href="#thm:formula" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="thm:formula">[thm:formula]</a> is finite and holds in expectation and in probability.
  - Explanation: Separate lower and upper clipping limits extend the formula to the stated integrable unbounded class.
- **C30** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-ext-growth)): Wholly unrestricted real activations need not have finite limiting pressure. For $f(s)=s^4$, choose $x_i=\operatorname{sign}(g_i^1)$. The law of large numbers gives $b_1(x)=N^{-1/2}\sum_i\|g_i^1\|\sim\sqrt{2/\pi}\sqrt N$ in probability. All other pattern contributions are nonnegative, and the probability weight of this one state is $2^{-N}$. Thus $p_N(f)\ge b_1(x)^4/N-\log2\to\infty$ in probability.
  - Explanation: An unrestricted quartic potential can yield divergent pressure through a configuration aligned with a pattern.
- **C31** (theorem, [source](#stmt-ext-sign)): Replace the Gaussian pattern entries by independent uniform signs in $\{-1,1\}$. If $f$ is bounded Borel and its discontinuity set has Gaussian measure zero, then its expected pressure and its pressure in probability converge to the same value $\mathcal P_f(\alpha)$ as in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">3</a>. In particular, this holds for every bounded continuous activation.
  - Explanation: Independent uniform sign patterns have the same limiting pressure when the bounded potential is continuous outside a Gaussian-null set.
- **C32** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-ext-sign-scope)): One cannot replace the hypothesis of Theorem <a href="#ext:sign" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:sign">34</a> by arbitrary bounded Borel measurability. Take $f=\mathbf1_{\mathbb Q}$. For Gaussian patterns, every field at every state is irrational almost surely, since there are only finitely many states and rows at each size; hence $p_N=0$. For sign patterns and square $N$, every field is rational, so $p_N=M_N/N$. For odd nonsquare $N$, the numerator of each field is a nonzero odd integer while $\sqrt N$ is irrational, so $p_N=0$. The two subsequences give distinct limits. This function is discontinuous everywhere and is excluded by Theorem <a href="#ext:sign" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:sign">34</a>.
  - Explanation: The rational-indicator potential distinguishes sign subsequences, so arbitrary bounded Borel measurability does not suffice for sign universality.

      "exact_statement": "For step paths on arbitrary partitions, $$\\begin{aligned}\n\\label{var:lipschitz}\n |V_f(p)-V_f(q)|&\\le\\tfrac12\\|f'\\|_\\infty^2\\|p-q\\|_{L^1},\\\\\n |\\ell(h')-\\ell(h)|&\\le\\tfrac12\\|h'-h\\|_{L^1}.\n\\end{aligned}$$ Thus $V_f$ extends uniquely and continuously to all nondecreasing $[0,1]$-valued paths, and $\\ell$ extends to all bounded nonnegative nondecreasing paths. Moreover, $$\\label{var:tail-order}\n \\int_s^1p(u)\\,du\\ge\\int_s^1q(u)\\,du\n \\quad\\hbox{for every }s\\in[0,1]\n \\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad V_f(p)\\le V_f(q).$$",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-arr-quantization",
        "exact_quote": "Let $B$ be nonnegative and ultrametric off the diagonal, with constant diagonal $D$ and entries at most $D$. Applying any nonnegative nondecreasing finite-valued map to its off-diagonal entries, with image contained in $[0,D]$, and retaining diagonal $D$, produces a Gram array. If $B$ has the Ghirlanda–Guerra property, its quantized off-diagonal law is the finite cascade law in Lemma <a href=\"#arr:cascade\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"arr:cascade\">11</a>.*\n\n*More generally, suppose finitely many desired off-diagonal covariance entries are bounded nonnegative nondecreasing functions $a_j(r)$ of the scalar entries $r=B_{\\ell m}$, $\\ell\\ne m$, with $a_j(r)\\leq D_j$. One can choose finite ordered bins of $r$ that approximate $r$ and every $a_j(r)$ uniformly on their domain, with errors tending to zero, while keeping each covariance diagonal equal to $D_j$. All the resulting covariance arrays are Gram. The binning can be chosen as a function of $r$ alone, so it never splits an atom of the scalar overlap law.",
        "source_id": "PM-222.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-arr-quantization",

        "anchor": "stmt-arr-law-continuity",
        "exact_quote": "For fixed diagonal $D$, the law of a nonnegative ultrametric Ghirlanda–Guerra array with values in $[0,D]$ is determined by its two-replica marginal.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-arr-tilt",
        "exact_quote": "Let $X_{k-1}$ be a terminal log factor depending on the marked path. For $i\\ge1$, let $\\mathbb E_i$ integrate the original level-$i$ child-mark kernel, conditional on the marked ancestor path through level $i-1$, without averaging the Poisson weights or root data. Define $$X_{i-1}=\\frac1{\\zeta_i}\\log\\mathbb E_i e^{\\zeta_i X_i},\n \\qquad i=k-1,\\ldots,1.\n \\label{arr:mark-recursion}$$ Assume these transforms are finite; the bounded log factors and the Gaussian log factors with at most linear growth used below satisfy this condition. For $Z=\\sum_\\gamma v_\\gamma e^{X_{k-1}(\\gamma)}$, $$\\mathbb E[\\log Z\\mid\\text{root data}]=X_0,\n \\qquad\n \\mathbb E[(\\log Z-X_0)^2\\mid\\text{root data}]\n \\leq4\\mathbb E[(\\log T)^2].\n \\label{arr:log-cascade}$$ Here $T$ is the unmarked total in <a href=\"#arr:cascade-weights\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"arr:cascade-weights\">[arr:cascade-weights]</a>.*\n\n\n*After reweighting by $e^{X_{k-1}}$ and relabeling children, the joint law of normalized weights and marks can be generated by first drawing an ordinary unmarked cascade, independently of the root data, and then drawing marks on its vertices with kernels of densities $$e^{\\zeta_i(X_i-X_{i-1})},\\qquad 1\\leq i\\leq k-1,\n \\label{arr:tilted-kernel}$$ relative to the original kernels, with independent draws on distinct children conditional on ancestor marks. Root data retain their original law. In particular, sampling reweighted leaves preserves their shape law; conditioning on that shape leaves exactly these mark kernels along the sampled branches. Independent mark components remain independent under these kernels when their original kernels factor and the terminal log factor is a sum of the separate component log factors.",
        "source_id": "PM-222.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-arr-tilt",
          "exact_quote": "Let $X_{k-1}$ be a terminal log factor depending on the marked path. For $Z=\\sum_\\gamma v_\\gamma e^{X_{k-1}(\\gamma)}$, $$\\mathbb E[\\log Z\\mid\\text{root data}]=X_0,\n \\qquad\n \\mathbb E[(\\log Z-X_0)^2\\mid\\text{root data}]\n \\leq4\\mathbb E[(\\log T)^2].\n \\label{arr:log-cascade}$$ Here $T$ is the unmarked total in <a href=\"#arr:cascade-weights\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"arr:cascade-weights\">[arr:cascade-weights]</a>.*\n\n\n*After reweighting by $e^{X_{k-1}}$ and relabeling children, the joint law of normalized weights and marks can be generated by first drawing an ordinary unmarked cascade, independently of the root data, and then drawing marks on its vertices with kernels of densities $$e^{\\zeta_i(X_i-X_{i-1})},\\qquad 1\\leq i\\leq k-1,\n \\label{arr:tilted-kernel}$$ relative to the original kernels, with independent draws on distinct children conditional on ancestor marks. Root data retain their original law. In particular, sampling reweighted leaves preserves their shape law; conditioning on that shape leaves exactly these mark kernels along the sampled branches. Independent mark components remain independent under these kernels when their original kernels factor and the terminal log factor is a sum of the separate component log factors.",
          "source_id": "PM-222.1"

For $i\\ge1$, let $\\mathbb E_i$ integrate the original level-$i$ child-mark kernel, conditional on the marked ancestor path through level $i-1$, without averaging the Poisson weights or root data. Define $$X_{i-1}=\\frac1{\\zeta_i}\\log\\mathbb E_i e^{\\zeta_i X_i},\n \\qquad i=k-1,\\ldots,1.\n \\label{arr:mark-recursion}$$ Assume these transforms are finite; the bounded log factors and the Gaussian log factors with at most linear growth used below satisfy this condition. For $Z=\\sum_\\gamma v_\\gamma e^{X_{k-1}(\\gamma)}$, $$\\mathbb E[\\log Z\\mid\\text{root data}]=X_0,\n \\qquad\n \\mathbb E[(\\log Z-X_0)^2\\mid\\text{root data}]\n \\leq4\\mathbb E[(\\log T)^2].\n \\label{arr:log-cascade}$$ Here $T$ is the unmarked total in <a href=\"#arr:cascade-weights\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"arr:cascade-weights\">[arr:cascade-weights]</a>.*\n\n\n*After reweighting by $e^{X_{k-1}}$ and relabeling children, the joint law of normalized weights and marks can be generated by first drawing an ordinary unmarked cascade, independently of the root data, and then drawing marks on its vertices with kernels of densities $$e^{\\zeta_i(X_i-X_{i-1})},\\qquad 1\\leq i\\leq k-1,\n \\label{arr:tilted-kernel}$$ relative to the original kernels, with independent draws on distinct children conditional on ancestor marks. Root data retain their original law. In particular, sampling reweighted leaves preserves their shape law; conditioning on that shape leaves exactly these mark kernels along the sampled branches. Independent mark components remain independent under these kernels when their original kernels factor and the terminal log factor is a sum of the separate component log factors.",
      "explanation": "Cascade reweighting changes the conditional mark kernels while preserving the unmarked shape law and the original root law.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-arr-limit",
        "exact_quote": "Let a sequence of random base probabilities have jointly convergent arrays of bounded observables and of the conditional covariances of finitely many fresh centered Gaussian fields. The fields are sampled independently of the base probability conditional on its data, with any specified independence between field components. Then the following averages converge, as determined by those limiting arrays:*\n\n1.  *bounded continuous functions of the listed observable and field evaluations on finitely many base replicas;*\n\n2.  *the same tests after reweighting by a fixed continuous function of the listed evaluations at one replica, bounded between two fixed positive constants;*\n\n3.  *the expected logarithm of the normalizing integral of that factor.*\n\n*Independent finite-spin averages may be included. The assertion remains valid at singular limiting covariance matrices. If the limiting off-diagonal covariance entries are ordered functions of a scalar ultrametric Ghirlanda–Guerra array, its evaluations can be obtained by the quantized cascades of Lemma <a href=\"#arr:quantization\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"arr:quantization\">12</a>, retaining the true diagonal variances and using <a href=\"#arr:residual-average\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"arr:residual-average\">[arr:residual-average]</a>, provided the base observables and tests used in the evaluation are also approximated by these bins in probability on every finite replica restriction. This condition includes constants and continuous tests of the scalar overlap and the quantized covariance coordinates.",
        "source_id": "PM-222.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-arr-limit",

The assertion remains valid at singular limiting covariance matrices.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-arr-fresh-pattern",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose that the limiting spin-overlap array of a sequence of base Gibbs probabilities has diagonal one, is Gram, and satisfies the Ghirlanda–Guerra property. Let $p$ be the quantile of its off-diagonal two-replica law. For a fresh independent Gaussian pattern field $g(x)$ with conditional covariance $R(x,x')$, and bounded continuous $f$, $$\\lim\\mathbb E\\log\\langle e^{f(g(x))}\\rangle=V_f(p),\n \\label{arr:fresh-pattern-value}$$ whenever $V_f$ is understood through its continuous quantile extension. In particular this applies to the smooth potentials used in the proof before the final approximation step.",
        "source_id": "PM-222.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

        }

        "anchor": "stmt-arr-calculus",
        "exact_quote": "Let $P$ be a probability reference measure, possibly random but independent of the Gaussian fields under consideration. For independent centered Gaussian energies with covariances $C_0,C_1$, interpolate using $H_t=\\sqrt{1-t}\\,H_0+\\sqrt t\\,H_1$, and put $\\Delta=C_1-C_0$. Then $$\\frac{d}{dt}\\mathbb E\\log P(e^{H_t})\n =\\frac12\\mathbb E\\langle\\Delta_{11}-\\Delta_{12}\\rangle_t.\n \\label{arr:gaussian-log-derivative}$$ For a bounded $r$-replica test $F$ independent of these fields, $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\frac{d}{dt}\\mathbb E\\langle F\\rangle_t\n =\\frac12\\mathbb E\\Bigg\\langle F\\Bigg(\n &\\sum_{a,b=1}^r\\Delta_{ab}\n -2r\\sum_{a=1}^r\\Delta_{a,r+1}\\notag\\\\\n &+r(r+1)\\Delta_{r+1,r+2}-r\\Delta_{r+1,r+1}\n \\Bigg)\\Bigg\\rangle_t,\n \\label{arr:replica-derivative}\n\\end{aligned}$$ so its magnitude is at most $(2r^2+r)\\|F\\|_\\infty\\|\\Delta\\|_\\infty$. If an independent centered Gaussian field $Y$ of covariance $C$ enters the energy with coefficient $s$, Gaussian integration by parts also gives $$\\mathbb E\\langle Y(1)F\\rangle\n =s\\,\\mathbb E\\left\\langle\n  F\\left(\\sum_{a=1}^r C_{1a}-rC_{1,r+1}\\right)\n \\right\\rangle.\n \\label{arr:gaussian-insertion}$$ These formulas hold for finite spin spaces with countably many cascade labels whenever the covariances are bounded at the fixed system size. They can be integrated up to the endpoints of covariance segments.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-arr-variance",
        "exact_quote": "A function of finite standard Gaussian data with Euclidean Lipschitz constant $A$ has variance at most $A^2$. A function of independent inputs whose value changes by at most $c_i$ when input $i$ is replaced has variance at most $\\sum_i c_i^2$. If a function $a$ on the integers has Lipschitz constant $c$ and $K$ is Poisson with mean $\\lambda$, then $\\operatorname{Var}(a(K))\\leq c^2\\lambda$.",
        "source_id": "PM-222.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "source_id": "PM-222.1"
        }

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-upper-concentration",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-upper-derivatives",
        "exact_quote": "The function $F_N$ is continuous on the parameter region above.         "anchor": "stmt-bulk-restoration",
        "exact_quote": "Start with any deterministic good parameter sequence, on any subsequence of dimensions. Pass to a further subsequence on which the omitted overlap array converges and the full system’s joint sampled arrays $(R_{lm},B_N(x^l,x^m),C_N(x^l))$ converge. Let $\\mu$ be the omitted limit’s two-replica overlap law. The full limiting overlap array has the same law as the omitted one. There are deterministic constants $a_{\\mathrm d},c$ and a deterministic nondecreasing function $a$, defined on a $\\mu$-full ordered subset of $[0,1]$, such that almost surely $$\\label{bulk:deterministic-covariances}\n B_{lm}=a(R_{lm})\\quad(l\\ne m),\\qquad\n B_{ll}=a_{\\mathrm d},\\qquad C_l=c,\n \\qquad 0\\le a\\le a_{\\mathrm d}\\le\\alpha\\|f'\\|_\\infty^2.$$ Moreover, $$\\label{bulk:ibp-balance}\n c+a_{\\mathrm d}=\\int r\\,a(r)\\,\\mu(dr).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-222.1"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cav-finite-comparison",
        "exact_quote": "Let $$\\label{cav:untilted-base}\n G_0=G_N^v\\otimes\\nu_L$$ be the size-$N$ bulk Gibbs probability times the independent cavity-spin prior, *before* multiplication by $W$. There is a deterministic $\\kappa_L>0$ such that $G_0(W)\\ge\\kappa_L$ for every realization and all sufficiently large $N$. Uniformly over all allowed $v$, $$\\label{cav:finite-increment}\n \\Delta_{N,L}(v)=\\mathbb E\\log G_0(W)+o(1).$$ The size-$(N+L)$ Gibbs expectations of any fixed bounded finite-replica spin test are likewise approximated, after the above coupling, by the measure $W\\,dG_0/G_0(W)$, with disorder-averaged error $o(1)$ times the test’s bound. In particular this applies to bounded continuous functions of $R^+_{12}$ and their products with $z_1^1z_1^2$; inside such continuous tests one may also replace $R^+_{12}$ by $R_{12}$. Finally, $\\Delta_{N,L}(v)\\ge-C_L$ uniformly for large $N$.",
        "source_id": "PM-222.1"
      },

In particular this applies to bounded continuous functions of $R^+_{12}$ and their products with $z_1^1z_1^2$; inside such continuous tests one may also replace $R^+_{12}$ by $R_{12}$. Finally, $\\Delta_{N,L}(v)\\ge-C_L$ uniformly for large $N$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-ext-path-approx",
        "exact_quote": "If $f,g$ are bounded Borel functions with $|f|,|g|\\le K$, then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\big|\\mathbb E p_N(f)-\\mathbb E p_N(g)\\big|\n &\\le\\alpha e^{2K}\\|f-g\\|_{L^1(\\gamma)},\\label{ext:pressure-distance}\\\\\n \\sup_{q\\text{ step}}|V_f(q)-V_g(q)|\n &\\le e^{2K}\\|f-g\\|_{L^1(\\gamma)}.\\label{ext:path-distance}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently, for each bounded Borel $f$, the pattern functional $V_f$ extends uniquely and continuously in $L^1$ from step paths to $\\mathcal Q$, and the bound in <a href=\"#ext:path-distance\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ext:path-distance\">[ext:path-distance]</a> holds on all of $\\mathcal Q$.",
        "source_id": "PM-222.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-ext-path-approx",
          "exact_quote": "If $f,g$ are bounded Borel functions with $|f|,|g|\\le K$, then $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\big|\\mathbb E p_N(f)-\\mathbb E p_N(g)\\big|\n &\\le\\alpha e^{2K}\\|f-g\\|_{L^1(\\gamma)},\\label{ext:pressure-distance}\\\\\n \\sup_{q\\text{ step}}|V_f(q)-V_g(q)|\n &\\le e^{2K}\\|f-g\\|_{L^1(\\gamma)}.\\label{ext:path-distance}\n\\end{aligned}$$ Consequently, for each bounded Borel $f$, the pattern functional $V_f$ extends uniquely and continuously in $L^1$ from step paths to $\\mathcal Q$, and the bound in <a href=\"#ext:path-distance\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"ext:path-distance\">[ext:path-distance]</a> holds on all of $\\mathcal Q$.",
          "source_id": "PM-222.1"
        }

        "exact_quote": "One cannot replace the hypothesis of Theorem <a href=\"#ext:sign\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ext:sign\">34</a> by arbitrary bounded Borel measurability. Take $f=\\mathbf1_{\\mathbb Q}$. For Gaussian patterns, every field at every state is irrational almost surely, since there are only finitely many states and rows at each size; hence $p_N=0$. For sign patterns and square $N$, every field is rational, so $p_N=M_N/N$. For odd nonsquare $N$, the numerator of each field is a nonzero odd integer while $\\sqrt N$ is irrational, so $p_N=0$. The two subsequences give distinct limits. This function is discontinuous everywhere and is excluded by Theorem <a href=\"#ext:sign\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"ext:sign\">34</a>.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Pattern and field recursions** ([source](#stmt-def-recursions)): For a step overlap path $q$, append $q_{-1}=0$ and $q_k=1$. Starting at $U_k=f$, set $$\label{def:pattern-recursion} U_{i-1}=\mathcal T_{q_i-q_{i-1},\zeta_i}U_i \quad(i=k,k-1,\ldots,0),\qquad V_f(q)=U_{-1}(0).$$ For a step field path $h$, append $h_{-1}=0$. Starting instead at $U_{k-1}(x)=\log\cosh x-h_{k-1}/2$, set $$\label{def:field-recursion} U_{i-1}=\mathcal T_{h_i-h_{i-1},\zeta_i}U_i \quad(i=k-1,k-2,\ldots,0),\qquad \ell(h)=U_{-1}(0).$$ The letter $U$ refers to the recursion under discussion.
- **Ising entropy** ([source](#stmt-def-entropy)): For $q\in\mathcal Q$, define the extended-valued functional $$\label{def:entropy-formula} S_{\mathrm I}(q)=\sup_{h\in\mathcal H_{\mathrm{step}}} \left\{\ell(h)+\frac12\int_0^1h(u)q(u)\,du\right\}.$$ The field partition is independent of the overlap partition. In calculations with two step paths, we always pass to a common refinement.
- **Ghirlanda–Guerra property** ([source](#stmt-arr-gg)): Let $B$ be a weakly exchangeable array with values in a compact space, and write $\mu=\mathcal L(B_{12})$. We say that $B$ has the Ghirlanda–Guerra property if, for every $n\geq2$, $$\mathcal L\bigl(B_{1,n+1}\mid (B_{\ell m})_{\ell,m\leq n}\bigr) =\frac1n\mu+\frac1n\sum_{\ell=2}^n\delta_{B_{1\ell}}. \label{arr:conditional-gg}$$ Equivalently, for every bounded measurable function $F$ of the first $n\times n$ block and every bounded measurable $\psi$, $$\mathbb E[F\psi(B_{1,n+1})] =\frac1n\mathbb E[F]\mathbb E[\psi(B_{12})] +\frac1n\sum_{\ell=2}^n\mathbb E[F\psi(B_{1\ell})]. \label{arr:gg-tested}$$ The same definition applies to a joint array, with $B_{\ell m}$ a tuple of entries. By exchangeability the distinguished index $1$ can be replaced by any of the first $n$ indices.
- **Overlap path** ([source](#pm-h-7)): An overlap path is a nondecreasing function $q:(0,1)\to[0,1]$.
- **Trial field path** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A trial field path is a nonnegative nondecreasing step function $h:(0,1)\to[0,\infty)$ with finite values.
- **Gibbs measure** ([source](#pm-h-8)): A Gibbs measure is a reference probability reweighted by an exponential energy and then normalized.
- **Replicas** ([source](#pm-h-8)): Replicas are independent samples from that measure conditional on its disorder and reference data.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-var-concavity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-var-concavity): Concavity and supporting fields -> A global supporting inequality
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-arr-ultrametric) / [proof](#proof-stmt-arr-ultrametric): Array duplication and Gram positivity -> Nonnegative ultrametric overlap arrays
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-upper-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-upper-synchronization-2): Enriched affine comparison -> Variational upper bound
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-bulk-restoration) / [proof](#proof-stmt-bulk-restoration): Independent pattern restoration and conditional moment identification -> Deterministic ordered gradient covariance
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-cav-dual-attainment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cav-dual-attainment): Coordinate exchangeability and supporting inequality -> Attainment of the entropy dual
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-cav-lower-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cav-lower-bound): Uniform increments and telescoping -> Matching variational lower bound
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-ext-bounded) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-bounded): Smooth approximation and concentration -> Bounded Borel formula in expectation and probability
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-ext-integrable) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-integrable): Ordered clipping and integrable tail control -> Integrable unbounded extension
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-ext-sign) / [proof](#proof-stmt-ext-sign): Entrywise replacement and Gaussian-null continuity -> Random-sign pressure universality

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I-pressure**: The Gaussian pressure converges in expectation and probability to the variational value.
- **I-quantization**: Ordered nonnegative quantization of an ultrametric array preserves the Gram property when the original diagonal is retained.
- **I-residual**: Repeated visits to a cascade leaf retain independent residual Gaussian noises.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A-capacity**: Use the pressure formula as a hard-constraint storage capacity certificate. -> A finite log-potential assigns positive weight to every configuration; hard constraints require a separate order-of-limits argument.
- **A-gram**: Quantize an arbitrary covariance matrix and assume it remains Gram. -> The threshold matrices need not encode nested equivalence classes.
- **A-residual**: Assign a permanent residual to each leaf. -> Repeated visits then share residual noise and acquire an incorrect off-diagonal covariance.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The ordered finite quantization and Gram decomposition are constructive for finite replica restrictions. This computation does not evaluate the thermodynamic variational infimum.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON list of at most 100 cases, rational matrix B and rational levels.
# PRECONDITIONS: matrix size 1..6, symmetric nonnegative ultrametric entries <= constant diagonal D; ordered levels starting at zero and <=D.
# OUTPUTS: quantized Gram matrices with original diagonal, or INVALID for rejected cases.
# TIME: O(cases*n^3 + cases*levels*n^2) exact arithmetic operations.
# SPACE: O(n^2 + levels) rationals per case, plus output.
# ARITHMETIC: exact python-flint fmpq; rational inputs are integers or p/q strings.
# FAILURE: invalid case returns INVALID; malformed JSON or case count outside contract raises.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpq

def construct(case):
    try:
        B = [[fmpq(v) for v in row] for row in case['B']]
        levels = [fmpq(v) for v in case['levels']]
        n = len(B)
        if not 1 <= n <= 6 or any(len(row) != n for row in B): return 'INVALID'
        D = B[0][0]
        if D < 0 or not levels or levels[0] != 0 or len(levels) > 20: return 'INVALID'
        if any(levels[i] >= levels[i+1] for i in range(len(levels)-1)) or levels[-1] > D: return 'INVALID'
        if any(B[i][i] != D for i in range(n)): return 'INVALID'
        if any(B[i][j] != B[j][i] or B[i][j] < 0 or B[i][j] > D for i in range(n) for j in range(n)): return 'INVALID'
        if any(B[i][j] < min(B[i][k], B[j][k]) for i in range(n) for j in range(n) for k in range(n) if len({i,j,k}) == 3): return 'INVALID'
        Q = [[max(c for c in levels if c <= B[i][j]) if i != j else D for j in range(n)] for i in range(n)]
        values = sorted({Q[i][j] for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if i != j}) or [fmpq(0)]
        R = [[values[0] for j in range(n)] for i in range(n)]
        for lower, upper in zip(values, values[1:]):
            for i in range(n):
                for j in range(n):
                    if i == j or Q[i][j] >= upper: R[i][j] += upper-lower
        for i in range(n): R[i][i] += D-values[-1]
        return [[str(v) for v in row] for row in R]
    except (KeyError, TypeError, ValueError, ZeroDivisionError):
        return 'INVALID'
cases = json.load(sys.stdin)
assert isinstance(cases,list) and 1 <= len(cases) <= 100
print(json.dumps([construct(case) for case in cases],separators=(',',':')))
```

    "oracle_code": "import sys, json, itertools\nfrom fractions import Fraction\nfrom flint import fmpq_mat\n\ndef decide(item):\n    try:\n        a = [[Fraction(x) for x in r] for r in item['B']]\n        cs = tuple(Fraction(x) for x in item['levels'])\n        size = len(a)\n        assert 0 < size < 7 and all(len(r)==size for r in a)\n        diagonal = a[0][0]\n        assert diagonal >= 0 and 0 < len(cs) <= 20 and cs[0]==0\n        assert list(cs)==sorted(set(cs)) and max(cs)<=diagonal\n        for u,v in itertools.product(range(size),repeat=2):\n            assert a[u][v]==a[v][u] and 0<=a[u][v]<=diagonal\n            if u==v: assert a[u][v]==diagonal\n        for u,v,w in itertools.combinations(range(size),3):\n            triple = sorted([a[u][v],a[u][w],a[v][w]])\n            assert triple[0]==triple[1]\n    except (AssertionError, ValueError, KeyError, TypeError, ZeroDivisionError):\n        return 'INVALID'\n    result = []\n    for u in range(size):\n        row=[]\n        for v in range(size):\n            if u==v: row.append(diagonal)\n            else:\n                bucket = sum(c<=a[u][v] for c in cs)-1\n                row.append(cs[bucket])\n        result.append(row)\n    for width in range(1,size+1):\n        for subset in itertools.combinations(range(size),width):\n            minor = fmpq_mat([[str(result[u][v]) for v in subset] for u in subset])\n            assert minor.det()>=0\n    return [[str(x) for x in r] for r in result]\ntry:\n    inputs=json.load(open(sys.argv[1]))\n    expected=[decide(item) for item in inputs]\n    supplied=json.load(open(sys.argv[2]))\n    declared=json.loads(sys.argv[3])\n    sys.exit(0 if supplied==expected==declared else 6)\nexcept Exception:\n    sys.exit(7)\n",
    "output_contract": "The off-diagonal entries are rounded down to the supplied ordered levels; the original diagonal is retained. Valid outputs are Gram matrices.",
    "reason": "The ordered finite quantization and Gram decomposition are constructive for finite replica restrictions. This computation does not evaluate the thermodynamic variational infimum.",
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON list of at most 100 cases, rational matrix B and rational levels.\n# PRECONDITIONS: matrix size 1..6, symmetric nonnegative ultrametric entries <= constant diagonal D; ordered levels starting at zero and <=D.\n# OUTPUTS: quantized Gram matrices with original diagonal, or INVALID for rejected cases.\n# TIME: O(cases*n^3 + cases*levels*n^2) exact arithmetic operations.\n# SPACE: O(n^2 + levels) rationals per case, plus output.\n# ARITHMETIC: exact python-flint fmpq; rational inputs are integers or p/q strings.\n# FAILURE: invalid case returns INVALID; malformed JSON or case count outside contract raises.\nimport json, sys\nfrom flint import fmpq\n\ndef construct(case):\n    try:\n        B = [[fmpq(v) for v in row] for row in case['B']]\n        levels = [fmpq(v) for v in case['levels']]\n        n = len(B)\n        if not 1 <= n <= 6 or any(len(row) != n for row in B): return 'INVALID'\n        D = B[0][0]\n        if D < 0 or not levels or levels[0] != 0 or len(levels) > 20: return 'INVALID'\n        if any(levels[i] >= levels[i+1] for i in range(len(levels)-1)) or levels[-1] > D: return 'INVALID'\n        if any(B[i][i] != D for i in range(n)): return 'INVALID'\n        if any(B[i][j] != B[j][i] or B[i][j] < 0 or B[i][j] > D for i in range(n) for j in range(n)): return 'INVALID'\n        if any(B[i][j] < min(B[i][k], B[j][k]) for i in range(n) for j in range(n) for k in range(n) if len({i,j,k}) == 3): return 'INVALID'\n        Q = [[max(c for c in levels if c <= B[i][j]) if i != j else D for j in range(n)] for i in range(n)]\n        values = sorted({Q[i][j] for i in range(n) for j in range(n) if i != j}) or [fmpq(0)]\n        R = [[values[0] for j in range(n)] for i in range(n)]\n        for lower, upper in zip(values, values[1:]):\n            for i in range(n):\n                for j in range(n):\n                    if i == j or Q[i][j] >= upper: R[i][j] += upper-lower\n        for i in range(n): R[i][i] += D-values[-1]\n        return [[str(v) for v in row] for row in R]\n    except (KeyError, TypeError, ValueError, ZeroDivisionError):\n        return 'INVALID'\ncases = json.load(sys.stdin)\nassert isinstance(cases,list) and 1 <= len(cases) <= 100\nprint(json.dumps([construct(case) for case in cases],separators=(',',':')))\n",

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |

| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The pressure theorem concerns a specified random spin model; a correspondence with software reliability or deployment behaviour is absent. |

| tools | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | The pressure normalization and explicit constant and linear activation checks can guide model-specific numerical diagnostics. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The ordered quantization lemma supplies a finite covariance construction on a finite replica restriction. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Nested threshold classes encode finite ultrametric covariances and preserve their Gram representation under ordered quantization. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper gives ensemble pressure and covariance statements; a mapping to accelerator architecture, timing, or memory costs is absent. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The ensemble limit supplies no cryptographic hardness reduction or adversarial security contract. |
| machine_learning | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Conditional application holds for asymptotic weighted binary perceptron models with independent Gaussian patterns and a bounded Borel log-potential at fixed positive density and temperature. |
| optimisation_operations_research | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | The variational principle and supporting-field inequality can guide analysis of the model-specific overlap-path objective. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Replica exchangeability and cascade sampling describe a random Gibbs ensemble; distributed replication or consensus guarantees are absent. |
| other (statistical_physics) | True | strong | adaptable | stated | The limiting pressure formula directly evaluates the positive-temperature Gaussian Ising perceptron, with the stated integrable and random-sign extensions. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The main Gaussian pressure formula assumes a uniform probability prior on binary configurations, independent standard Gaussian patterns, fixed positive density, and a bounded Borel real log-potential. Blocked use: ['Applying the formula to dependent patterns or a different spin prior without a separate proof.']
- **L2** (edge_case): Hard constraints and zero-temperature storage thresholds require additional control of the order of limits; finite positive-temperature pressure does not itself establish storage capacity. Blocked use: ['Reading the pressure formula as a hard-constraint capacity theorem.']
- **L3** (nonconstructive): The main result gives a limiting ensemble pressure in expectation and probability, rather than an instance-level optimization algorithm or a worst-case guarantee. Blocked use: ['Claiming a solver runtime or deployment reliability guarantee from the limiting variational value.']
- **L4** (approximation): Finite step trials suffice for the variational infimum, but this reduction does not supply a finite discretization error bound or continuity of the extended-valued entropy. Blocked use: ['Treating a finite path discretization as a certified pressure evaluation without additional error control.']
- **L5** (hypothesis): Ultrametricity and nonnegative off-diagonal overlaps require the stated Gram, exchangeability, constant-diagonal and Ghirlanda–Guerra assumptions. Blocked use: ['Applying overlap-array conclusions to an arbitrary similarity matrix or a software reliability model.']
- **L6** (approximation): Array-law continuity concerns fixed finite replica blocks and supplies no rate uniform in block size. Blocked use: ['Using the continuity statement as a block-size-independent quantitative error certificate.']
- **L7** (edge_case): Residual Gaussian noise belongs to each replica visit, including repeated visits to the same cascade leaf. Blocked use: ['Assigning one permanent residual to a leaf when reconstructing off-diagonal covariance.']
- **L8** (hypothesis): Passage through normalized array limits requires the bounded positive reweighting assumptions; unbounded factors need a separate uniform-integrability argument. Blocked use: ['Passing arbitrary unbounded reweightings through the array limit.']
- **L9** (edge_case): The cavity dimension is fixed while taking the bulk dimension limit, before increasing the number of cavity coordinates. Blocked use: ['Interchanging the cavity and bulk limits without a uniform comparison.']
- **L10** (hypothesis): The restored covariance and cavity calculations use smooth compactly supported log-potentials before the final approximation step. Blocked use: ['Applying the derivative-based empirical covariance formulas directly to arbitrary bounded Borel potentials.']
- **L11** (approximation): Entropy attainment retains bins that are functions of the scalar overlap, so flat overlap intervals must not be split. Blocked use: ['Identifying the full derivative path from overlap tests after splitting a constant-overlap interval.']
- **L12** (hypothesis): The unbounded extension requires integrability of the absolute log-potential and its exponential under the standard Gaussian law, with the clipping limits taken in the stated order. Blocked use: ['Extending the formula to arbitrary unbounded activations or reversing the clipping limits without further estimates.']
- **L13** (counterexample_scope): An unrestricted real activation can have divergent pressure; the quartic activation exhibits this obstruction. Blocked use: ['Assuming every real activation has a finite limiting variational pressure.']
- **L14** (hypothesis): Random-sign universality requires bounded Borel potentials whose discontinuity set has Gaussian measure zero. Blocked use: ['Transferring the Gaussian formula to every bounded Borel potential for sign patterns.']
- **L15** (counterexample_scope): The rational-indicator potential gives different subsequential pressures for sign patterns and is excluded by the universality hypothesis. Blocked use: ['Dropping the Gaussian-null discontinuity condition in sign universality.']

>
> - [The free energy of the Ising random perceptron](../../preprints/The-free-energy-of-the-Ising-random-perceptron-September-24-2026/The-free-energy-of-the-Ising-random-perceptron-September-24-2026.pdf)
> - [The free energy of the spherical random perceptron](../../preprints/The-free-energy-of-the-spherical-random-perceptron-September-24-2026/The-free-energy-of-the-spherical-random-perceptron-September-24-2026.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The linked formalization proves finiteness of the variational value used for the Ising random perceptron. For every nonnegative pattern density and every bounded continuous log-potential, the infimum over admissible overlap paths is a finite real number.
>
> This is a supporting well-definedness result. It does not assert convergence of the finite-system pressure or the paper's extension to all bounded Borel log-potentials.
>
> The formalization proves the variational formula for the spherical random perceptron's limiting pressure for every positive pattern density and inverse temperature and every bounded continuous single-pattern potential. It shows that the variational value is finite and that the pressure converges to it both in expectation and in probability.
>
> The linked spherical-field result supplies a supporting dual formula: finite hierarchy field values converge uniformly on compact parameter sets to an attained dual minimum, and the corresponding bounded stationary-field approximation holds for monotone overlap quantiles bounded away from one.

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

open MeasureTheory ProbabilityTheory Filter
open scoped BigOperators Topology ENNReal

noncomputable section
namespace IsingPerceptron

def gaussianTransform (s d : ℝ) (U : ℝ → ℝ) (x : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  if d = 0 then ∫ z, U (x + Real.sqrt s * z) ∂gaussianReal 0 1
  else (Real.log (∫ z, Real.exp (d * U (x + Real.sqrt s * z))
    ∂gaussianReal 0 1)) / d

structure Partition where
  levels : ℕ
  positive : 0 < levels
  cut : Fin (levels + 1) → ℝ
  ordered : StrictMono cut
  first : cut ⟨0, Nat.zero_lt_succ levels⟩ = 0
  last : cut ⟨levels, Nat.lt_succ_self levels⟩ = 1

structure FieldStep where
  partition : Partition
  value : Fin partition.levels → ℝ
  nonneg : ∀ i, 0 ≤ value i
  ordered : Monotone value

 
def stepAt (h : FieldStep) (i : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  if hi : i < h.partition.levels then h.value ⟨i, hi⟩ else 0

 

def stepFunction (h : FieldStep) (u : ℝ) : ℝ :=
  ∑ i : Fin h.partition.levels,
    if h.partition.cut i.castSucc < u ∧ u < h.partition.cut i.succ then h.value i else 0

 
def fieldRecursion (h : FieldStep) : ℝ :=
  let k := h.partition.levels
  let increments := List.ofFn (fun i : Fin k =>
    (stepAt h i - (if i.val = 0 then 0 else stepAt h (i.val - 1)),
      h.partition.cut i.castSucc))
  increments.foldr (fun sd U => gaussianTransform sd.1 sd.2 U)
    (fun x => Real.log (Real.cosh x) - stepAt h (k - 1) / 2) 0

def pathMeasure : Measure ℝ := volume.restrict (Set.Ioo (0 : ℝ) 1)

 
structure OverlapPath where
  val : ℝ →ₘ[pathMeasure] ℝ
  admissible : ∃ q : ℝ → ℝ,
    (q =ᵐ[pathMeasure] val) ∧ MonotoneOn q (Set.Ioo 0 1) ∧
    ∀ u ∈ Set.Ioo (0 : ℝ) 1, q u ∈ Set.Icc (0 : ℝ) 1

 
def cellAverage (q : OverlapPath) (k i : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  (k : ℝ) * ∫ u in Set.Ioo ((i : ℝ) / k) (((i : ℝ) + 1) / k),
    q.val u ∂pathMeasure

 

def uniformPattern (f : ℝ → ℝ) (q : OverlapPath) (n : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  let k := n + 1
  let levelVal := fun i => if i < k then cellAverage q k i else 1
  let increments := List.ofFn (fun i : Fin (k + 1) =>
    (levelVal i - (if i.val = 0 then 0 else levelVal (i.val - 1)), (i : ℝ) / k))
  increments.foldr (fun sd U => gaussianTransform sd.1 sd.2 U) f 0

 

def patternFunctional (f : ℝ → ℝ) (q : OverlapPath) : ℝ :=
  Filter.limUnder atTop (uniformPattern f q)

 

def isingEntropy (q : OverlapPath) : EReal :=
  ⨆ h : FieldStep,
    ((fieldRecursion h + (∫ u, stepFunction h u * q.val u ∂pathMeasure) / 2 : ℝ) : EReal)

def variationalValue (α : ℝ) (f : ℝ → ℝ) : EReal :=
  ⨅ q : OverlapPath, (α * patternFunctional f q : ℝ) + isingEntropy q

theorem variationalValue_real_of_continuous {α : ℝ} (hα : 0 ≤ α)
    {f : ℝ → ℝ} (hf : Continuous f) (hbounded : ∃ K : ℝ, ∀ x, |f x| ≤ K) :
    ∃ p : ℝ, variationalValue α f = (p : EReal) := by
  sorry

end IsingPerceptron
end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We determine the limiting free energy of the Ising perceptron with independent Gaussian patterns for every bounded Borel log-potential, at every fixed positive temperature and pattern density. We give an explicit variational formula for the limit and prove convergence in expectation and probability.
