Integration proves <a href="#ext:pressure-distance" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:pressure-distance">[ext:pressure-distance]</a>.

For a fixed step path, terminal interpolation in the recursion propagates $g-f$ by its tilted Gaussian kernels. This differentiation requires only bounded terminal functions: it follows directly by differentiating the finite expectations. In the notation of the pattern recursion, the density of the entire tilted path relative to its independent Gaussian increments has logarithm <a id="ext:telescoped-density"></a>$$\label{ext:telescoped-density}
 \sum_{i=0}^{k}\zeta_i\big(U_i(Z_i)-U_{i-1}(Z_{i-1})\big)
 =f_t(Z_k)-\sum_{i=0}^{k-1}w_iU_i(Z_i),
 \qquad f_t=(1-t)f+tg.$$ All the functions on the right take values in $[-K,K]$, so the density is at most $e^{2K}$. Without the tilt, $Z_k$ is standard normal, since the total variance is $q_k-q_{-1}=1$. This proves <a href="#ext:path-distance" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:path-distance">[ext:path-distance]</a>, uniformly over partitions and path values.

Choose $f_j\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R)$ with $|f_j|\le K$ and $\|f_j-f\|_{L^1(\gamma)}\to0$. Such approximations follow by spatial truncation, approximation on compact intervals, and mollification with a nonnegative smooth kernel. Each $V_{f_j}$ is continuous in $L^1$ by Proposition <a href="#var:continuity-order" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="var:continuity-order">5</a>. Estimate <a href="#ext:path-distance" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:path-distance">[ext:path-distance]</a> makes them uniformly Cauchy on the dense set of step paths and hence on $\mathcal Q$. Their uniform limit is continuous and agrees with the original finite recursion on every step path. Density also proves uniqueness. The elementary bound $|V_f|\le\|f\|_\infty$ persists. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-ext-bounded"></a>
<div id="ext:bounded" class="proposition">

**Proposition 31** (Completion of the bounded case). *Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">3</a> holds for every bounded Borel activation.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-ext-bounded"><summary>Proof of stmt-ext-bounded</summary>

*Proof.* Use the approximants from Lemma <a href="#ext:path-approx" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:path-approx">30</a>. Since the entropy functional is the same for every activation, $$|\mathcal P_f(\alpha)-\mathcal P_{f_j}(\alpha)|
 \le\alpha\sup_{q\in\mathcal Q}|V_f(q)-V_{f_j}(q)|\longrightarrow0.$$ Together with the uniform pressure estimate, the already proved smooth formula gives convergence of $\mathbb E p_N(f)$ to $\mathcal P_f(\alpha)$.

<a id="ext:concentration"></a>
Changing a single whole pattern vector changes $\log Z_N(f)$ by at most $2\|f\|_\infty$. The independent-input variance bound of Lemma <a href="#arr:variance" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="arr:variance">18</a> therefore gives $$\label{ext:concentration}
 \operatorname{Var}(p_N(f))\le
 \frac{4M_N\|f\|_\infty^2}{N^2}=O(N^{-1}).$$ Chebyshev’s inequality proves convergence in probability. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-37"></a>
#### An integrable unbounded class

The factor $e^{2K}$ in the bounded approximation estimate is not useful when the clipping level tends to infinity. Instead, we control the two tails separately: integrability of $f$ controls the lower tail, while integrability of $e^f$ controls the upper tail after a fixed lower clip. This is why the limits below are taken in the stated order.

<a id="stmt-ext-integrable"></a>
<div id="ext:integrable" class="theorem">

<a id="ext:moments"></a>
<a id="ext:unbounded-V"></a>
**Theorem 32** (Integrable activations). *Suppose $f:\mathbb R\to\mathbb R$ is Borel and $$\label{ext:moments}
 \mathbb E|f(G)|+\mathbb E e^{f(G)}<\infty.$$ For $J,K>0$, let $f_{J,K}=\min\{K,\max\{-J,f\}\}$. The uniform limit $$\label{ext:unbounded-V}
 V_f=\lim_{J\to\infty}\lim_{K\to\infty}V_{f_{J,K}}
 \quad\text{on }\mathcal Q$$ exists and is continuous in $L^1$. With this definition, the variational formula <a href="#thm:formula" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="thm:formula">[thm:formula]</a> is finite and holds in expectation and in probability.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-ext-integrable"><summary>Proof of stmt-ext-integrable</summary>

<a id="ext:fresh-marginal"></a>
*Proof.* We establish estimates uniform in both the dimension and the step path. Let $P$ be the base probability for one fresh pattern, and let $t$ be the value of $f$ on its field. In the spin model, $P$ is the Gibbs measure with that whole pattern omitted, independent of the fresh row. For a step path, let $(v_\lambda)$ be the cascade weights and let $(y_\lambda)$ be the Gaussian field built from shared tree increments, independent of those weights and with variance $q_{k-1}$ at each leaf. Use the product probability and scalar function $$P=\sum_\lambda v_\lambda\delta_\lambda\otimes\gamma,
 \qquad
 t(\lambda,\eta)=f\bigl(y_\lambda+\sqrt{1-q_{k-1}}\,\eta\bigr).$$ Thus the residual $\eta$ is an integration coordinate of $P$, as in Lemma <a href="#arr:fresh-pattern" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="arr:fresh-pattern">16</a>. For a bounded Borel terminal function, integrating $\eta$ gives the coefficient-one transform, and Lemma <a href="#arr:tilt" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="arr:tilt">14</a>, followed by the root Gaussian average, evaluates the expected log integral as its pattern recursion. This identity uses measurability and boundedness, not the continuity needed for an array limit. Zero residual variance and repeated covariance levels are allowed. In the spin case, the fresh field at each fixed state is standard normal. In the cascade case the same marginal results after both averaging $y_\lambda$ and integrating $\eta$. Independence of the weights and the shared field, followed by Tonelli’s theorem, therefore gives, for every nonnegative Borel $F$, $$\label{ext:fresh-marginal}
 \mathbb E P[F(t)]=\mathbb E F(f(G)).$$ Here the expectation includes the independent pattern and any base data. The log integral $\log P(e^t)$ is finite almost surely and integrable: Jensen bounds it below by $P(t)$, while its positive part is bounded by $P(e^t)$. These are integrable by <a href="#ext:moments" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:moments">[ext:moments]</a> and <a href="#ext:fresh-marginal" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:fresh-marginal">[ext:fresh-marginal]</a>. Adding the rows successively also proves integrability of the finite-volume pressure.

Put $t_J=\max(t,-J)$ and $r=(-J-t)_+$. The nonnegative lower-clipping error satisfies <a id="ext:lower-clip"></a>$$\begin{aligned}
 0\le \log P(e^{t_J})-\log P(e^t)
 &=-\log\frac{P(e^{t_J}e^{-r})}{P(e^{t_J})}
 \le\frac{P(e^{t_J}r)}{P(e^{t_J})}
 \le P(r).\label{ext:lower-clip}
\end{aligned}$$ The bound by the tilted mean of $r$ is Jensen’s inequality under the $e^{t_J}$ tilt. For the last inequality, $e^{\max(t,-J)}$ is increasing in $t$, while $(-J-t)_+$ is decreasing. Their covariance under $P$ is nonpositive. This follows also by expanding the expectation of $(a(t)-a(t'))(b(t)-b(t'))\le0$ for two independent $P$ samples.

<a id="ext:unbounded-pressure-error"></a>
<a id="ext:unbounded-path-error"></a>
After the lower clip, the upper-clipped exponential is at least $e^{-J}$. The inequality $\log(1+x)\le x$ gives <a id="ext:upper-clip"></a>$$\label{ext:upper-clip}
 0\le \log P(e^{t_J})-\log P(e^{\min(t_J,K)})
 \le e^J P[(e^t-e^K)_+].$$ Define $$\varepsilon_{J,K}=
 \mathbb E(-J-f(G))_+
 +e^J\mathbb E(e^{f(G)}-e^K)_+.$$ Replacing one pattern at a time in the finite spin system, allowing the other factors to be arbitrary existing clipped or unclipped factors, yields $$\label{ext:unbounded-pressure-error}
 \mathbb E|p_N(f)-p_N(f_{J,K})|\le\alpha\varepsilon_{J,K}.$$ The omitted factor is independent of that base probability at each replacement, so <a href="#ext:fresh-marginal" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:fresh-marginal">[ext:fresh-marginal]</a> continues to apply. The same argument for the single cascade log integral yields $$\label{ext:unbounded-path-error}
 \sup_{q\text{ step}}|V_f^{\mathrm{cas}}(q)-V_{f_{J,K}}(q)|
 \le\varepsilon_{J,K},$$ where $V_f^{\mathrm{cas}}(q)$ denotes its integrable expected log value.

At fixed $J$, the upper-clipping bound compares each bounded recursion uniformly on step paths with the cascade value for $\max(f,-J)$; its error tends to zero as $K\to\infty$. Hence these bounded continuous functionals are uniformly Cauchy on the dense set of step paths and therefore on $\mathcal Q$. Their uniform limit is continuous. For $J'\ge J$, apply the lower-clipping bound with $f$ replaced by $\max(f,-J')$. The difference between the corresponding limits is at most $\mathbb E(-J-f(G))_+$, which tends to zero as $J\to\infty$. This proves the ordered uniform limit <a href="#ext:unbounded-V" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:unbounded-V">[ext:unbounded-V]</a>. It agrees with $V_f^{\mathrm{cas}}$ on step paths, and <a href="#ext:unbounded-path-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:unbounded-path-error">[ext:unbounded-path-error]</a>, with $V_f$ on the left, passes to all paths by density.

Two applications of Jensen’s inequality to the same cascade integral give $$\mathbb E f(G)\le V_f(q)\le\log\mathbb E e^{f(G)}.$$ The bounds hold first for step paths and then for every $q\in\mathcal Q$. At $q=0$ the upper bound is an equality. Since $S_{\mathrm I}\ge0$ and $S_{\mathrm I}(0)=0$, the variational value is finite, with $$\alpha\mathbb E f(G)\le\mathcal P_f(\alpha)
 \le\alpha\log\mathbb E e^{f(G)}.$$ Estimate <a href="#ext:unbounded-path-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:unbounded-path-error">[ext:unbounded-path-error]</a> also bounds the difference between the clipped and unclipped variational infima by $\alpha\varepsilon_{J,K}$.

Apply Proposition <a href="#ext:bounded" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:bounded">31</a> to $f_{J,K}$ and then <a href="#ext:unbounded-pressure-error" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:unbounded-pressure-error">[ext:unbounded-pressure-error]</a>. This proves convergence in expectation. For convergence in probability, Markov’s inequality controls $p_N(f)-p_N(f_{J,K})$ uniformly in $N$; convergence for the bounded model handles the middle term. Take $N\to\infty$, then $K\to\infty$ at fixed $J$, and finally $J\to\infty$. No variance estimate for the unbounded model is required. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-ext-growth"></a>
<div id="ext:growth" class="remark">

*Remark 33* (Why some activation restriction is needed). Wholly unrestricted real activations need not have finite limiting pressure. For $f(s)=s^4$, choose $x_i=\operatorname{sign}(g_i^1)$. The law of large numbers gives $b_1(x)=N^{-1/2}\sum_i|g_i^1|\sim\sqrt{2/\pi}\sqrt N$ in probability. All other pattern contributions are nonnegative, and the probability weight of this one state is $2^{-N}$. Thus $p_N(f)\ge b_1(x)^4/N-\log2\to\infty$ in probability.

</div>

<a id="pm-h-38"></a>
#### Universality for random-sign patterns

Disorder universality has also been established for broad hard-constraint Ising perceptron classes by Nakajima and Sun (Nakajima and Sun 2023) <a id="pm-cite-47"></a>[[nakajimasun2022](#ref-nakajimasun2022)]. The following result concerns finite bounded log-potentials and records the continuity assumption used in the sign-to-Gaussian passage.

<a id="stmt-ext-sign"></a>
<div id="ext:sign" class="theorem">

**Theorem 34** (Random-sign patterns). *Replace the Gaussian pattern entries by independent uniform signs in $\{-1,1\}$. If $f$ is bounded Borel and its discontinuity set has Gaussian measure zero, then its expected pressure and its pressure in probability converge to the same value $\mathcal P_f(\alpha)$ as in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">3</a>. In particular, this holds for every bounded continuous activation.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-ext-sign"><summary>Proof of stmt-ext-sign</summary>

*Proof.* First let $f\in C_c^\infty(\mathbb R)$. We use the coordinate-replacement form of the Lindeberg principle; see Chatterjee (Chatterjee 2006, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-48"></a>[[chatterjee2006lindeberg](#ref-chatterjee2006lindeberg)]. Regard normalized pressure as a function of a single pattern entry. Three differentiations of the finite spin sum give a uniform bound $C_fN^{-5/2}$ for its third derivative. Indeed, each derivative of the pattern argument supplies $x_i/\sqrt N$, and the normalized log partition supplies the factor $1/N$; the remaining terms are bounded Gibbs moments and cumulants of $f',f'',f'''$. Taylor expansion through degree two and matching of the first two Gaussian and sign moments therefore bound each entry replacement by $C_fN^{-5/2}$. There are $M_NN=O(N^2)$ entries, so replacing them one at a time gives an expected-pressure error $O_f(N^{-1/2})$.

<a id="ext:sign-approx"></a>
For a general $f$ as in the statement, take smooth approximants $f_j$ with $|f_j|,|f|\le K$ and $\|f_j-f\|_{L^1(\gamma)}\to0$. The omitted-pattern argument from Lemma <a href="#ext:path-approx" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:path-approx">30</a> still applies. Conditional on any hypercube state, however, the fresh sign field now has the common law $$X_N=N^{-1/2}\sum_{i=1}^N\epsilon_i,
 \qquad \mathbb P(\epsilon_i=1)=\mathbb P(\epsilon_i=-1)=1/2.$$ Consequently, $$\label{ext:sign-approx}
 \big|\mathbb E p_N^{\mathrm{sign}}(f)
       -\mathbb E p_N^{\mathrm{sign}}(f_j)\big|
 \le\alpha e^{2K}\mathbb E|f(X_N)-f_j(X_N)|.$$ The same third-order replacement calculation on smooth compactly supported tests, together with $\mathbb EX_N^2=1$, proves $X_N\Rightarrow G$. The bounded function $|f-f_j|$ is continuous outside a Gaussian-null set. The usual bounded almost-everywhere-continuous test criterion for weak convergence therefore gives $$\mathbb E|f(X_N)-f_j(X_N)|
 \longrightarrow\mathbb E|f(G)-f_j(G)|.$$ Take $N\to\infty$ in <a href="#ext:sign-approx" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:sign-approx">[ext:sign-approx]</a> for fixed $j$, use the smooth replacement result and the Gaussian formula, and then take $j\to\infty$. This proves expected-pressure convergence. The bounded-pattern variance bound <a href="#ext:concentration" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:concentration">[ext:concentration]</a> holds for independent sign rows as well, which proves convergence in probability. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-ext-sign-scope"></a>
<div id="ext:sign-scope" class="remark">

*Remark 35* (The regularity condition in sign universality). One cannot replace the hypothesis of Theorem <a href="#ext:sign" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:sign">34</a> by arbitrary bounded Borel measurability. Take $f=\mathbf1_{\mathbb Q}$. For Gaussian patterns, every field at every state is irrational almost surely, since there are only finitely many states and rows at each size; hence $p_N=0$. For sign patterns and square $N$, every field is rational, so $p_N=M_N/N$. For odd nonsquare $N$, the numerator of each field is a nonzero odd integer while $\sqrt N$ is irrational, so $p_N=0$. The two subsequences give distinct limits. This function is discontinuous everywhere and is excluded by Theorem <a href="#ext:sign" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="ext:sign">34</a>.

</div>

<a id="pm-h-39"></a>
#### Normalization checks

For $f=c$ constant, $V_f=c$ and $\inf_qS_{\mathrm I}(q)=0$, so <a href="#thm:formula" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="thm:formula">[thm:formula]</a> gives $\alpha c$, as the partition function does directly. A second check exercises both the diagonal shift and the duality. For $f(s)=ts$, condition <a href="#ext:moments" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="ext:moments">[ext:moments]</a> holds, and the finite Gaussian recursion gives $$V_f(q)=\frac{t^2}{2}\left(1-\int_0^1q(u)\,du\right).$$ Set $H=\alpha t^2$. The constant field $h=H$ is an admissible trial, so every variational objective is at least $H/2+\ell(H)$. For the constant path $q_*=\mathbb E\tanh^2(\sqrt H\,G)$, the supporting inequality <a href="#var:support" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="var:support">[var:support]</a> shows that the entropy supremum is attained at that constant field. Equality follows, giving $$\mathcal P_{ts}(\alpha)
 =H/2+\ell(H)=\mathbb E\log\cosh(\sqrt H\,G).$$ This is also the direct answer: the linear activation produces independent Gaussian coordinate fields with variances $t^2M_N/N\to H$, and the Ising partition function factors into their $\cosh$ terms.

  "figures": [
    {
      "caption": "A two-leaf schematic of the Gaussian construction before reweighting. Shared increments belong to tree vertices, while residual noises belong to replica visits. The centered Gaussian residuals $ϵ_{1}, ϵ_{2}, ϵ_{3}$ are independent and have variance $D − b_{k − 1}$, even though visits $1$ and $2$ have the same leaf label.",
      "figure_id": "arr:residual-figure",
      "pdf_page": 18,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "arr:residual-figure",
          "exact_quote": "A two-leaf schematic of the Gaussian construction before\n reweighting. Shared increments\n belong to tree vertices, while residual noises belong to replica visits.\n The centered Gaussian residuals $\\epsilon_1,\\epsilon_2,\\epsilon_3$ are\n inde",
          "source_object": "fig:0001"
        }
      ],
      "source_kind": "tikz_pgf",
      "tikz_pgf_source": "\\begin{tikzpicture}[>=Stealth, every node/.style={font=\\small},\n                     shared/.style={draw,rounded corners,inner sep=5pt},\n                     visit/.style={draw,dashed,rounded corners,inner sep=5pt}]\n  \\node[shared] (root) at (0,0) {common root $Z_0$};\n  \\node[shared] (left) at (-2,-1.25) {leaf $\\gamma$: $y_\\gamma$};\n  \\node[shared] (right) at (2,-1.25) {leaf $\\eta$: $y_\\eta$};\n  \\node[visit] (vone) at (-3.3,-2.6) {visit $1$: $y_\\gamma+\\epsilon_1$};\n  \\node[visit] (vtwo) at (-.1,-2.6) {visit $2$: $y_\\gamma+\\epsilon_2$};\n  \\node[visit] (vthree) at (3.3,-2.6) {visit $3$: $y_\\eta+\\epsilon_3$};\n  \\draw[->] (root)--(left);\n  \\draw[->] (root)--(right);\n  \\draw[->,dashed] (left)--(vone);\n  \\draw[->,dashed] (left)--(vtwo);\n  \\draw[->,dashed] (right)--(vthree);\n \\end{tikzpicture}",
      "witness_description": "A directed tree diagram arranged vertically in three levels. At the top level is a solid rectangular box labeled \"common root $Z_0$\". Two solid downward arrows emerge from the root box: the left arrow points to a solid rectangular box labeled \"leaf $\\gamma$: $y_\\gamma$\", and the right arrow points to a solid rectangular box labeled \"leaf $\\eta$: $y_\\eta$\". From the \"leaf $\\gamma$: $y_\\gamma$\" box, two dashed downward arrows lead to two dashed rectangular boxes on the bottom level labeled \"visit 1: $y_\\gamma + \\epsilon_1$\" and \"visit 2: $y_\\gamma + \\epsilon_2$\". From the \"leaf $\\eta$: $y_\\eta$\" box, a single dashed downward arrow leads to a dashed rectangular box on the bottom level labeled \"visit 3: $y_\\eta + \\epsilon_3$\"."
    }
  ],
